pages  or  otherwise  damaging  it,  will

offence;  Unless  a borrower  points  out 
the  Injury  at  the  time  of  borrowing  the

I

Ki  PURISKAM,  Ki  PURINAM,  Ki  PAROM  bad  Ki 
KHANATANG  ki  don  sawdong  kanc  ka  ri  jong  ngi,  ha 
ki  Trep  bad  ki  Skum,  sawdong  ka  Lyngwiar  Dpci  ba  syaid 
bad  ba  rhem  jong  U Khun  Khasi  Pnar  baroh.  Hynrci 
ka  long  kaba  eh  shibun  ban  lum  ban  lang,  bad  ban  tai  ban 
peh  bha  ia  ki  shuwa  ban  pynsah  ia  ki  ha  ka  thoh  ka  tar 
kum  ki  Mar  Kynti  ka  ri.  Kam  long  ka  kam  kaba  suk 
ban  lum  na  kaba  shu  iohsngew  ka  shkor,  bad  kaba  shu 
kem  na  ka  hamsaian  shaphang  kiei  kiei  kiba  ngi  ju 
iohsngew  bad  ia  kren  barabor  hapdeng  jong  ngi,  kum  ia  ki 
Khlawait,  ki  Thma,  ki  Kut,  ki  Kharai  bad  ka  jingngeit  ki 
briew  ia  ki  Ryngkew  ki  Basa  kiba  don  sawdong  ki  Lum  bad 
ki  Wah  jong  ngi.

La  lum  ia  kine  ki  Khanatang,  khyndiat  nangne  bad 
khyndiat  nangtai,shi  kyntien  na  une  bad  shi  kyntien  na  utai. 
La  donkam  ban  trei  minot  rot  shi  rot  bad  slem  slem  eh. 
La  dei  ruh  ban  thaifi  ia  pra  bun  bun  wat.  Khyndiat  eh 
kiba  sngcwthuh  ia  ka  jingtrei  shitom  ki  Nonglum  kum 
ia  kine  kiei  kiei  ha  kane  ka  ri  jong  ngi.

Ka  don  ka  jingitynnad  ha  kine  ki  Khanatang  ba  la 
lum  na  sawdong  ka  ri,  namar  ba  ki  don  ki  Kyrteng  jong 
ki  Wah,  ki  Shnong  bad  ki  Jaka,  lem  bad  ki  Kyrteng  jong 
ki  briew  kiba  la  sdang  ban  jah  rngai  noh  na  ka  jingkynmaw 
bad  jingniewkor  jong  ka  Pateng  kaba  dang  khie. v Kine 
kin  sa  nang  jah  irat  lymda  ngi  lum  kloi  bad  pynsah  ia  ki  ha 
ka  thoh  ka  tar.  Ka  bor  jong  ka  Kyrteng  ka  long  kaba  phylla. 
Namar  kata,  u Khasi  u ong,  *£/  long  Soh  tong  Syntiew ’ haba 
ym  pat  jer  ia  u Khyllung.  Hynrei,  shisien  ba  la  jer  ia  tt 
ha  khmat  u Klong  u Skaw,  u im  ka  Kyrteng  bad  sah  ka  Nam 
shaduh  bymjukut.

11

SHI  KYNTIEN

Nalor  kata  ruh,  kine  ki  Khanatang  ki  long  kiba 
sngewtynnad  ha  kajinglyngkot  jong  ki.  Haba  pule  ia  ki  ngim 
lah  khlem  da  sngewkwah  ban  leit  jngoh  ia  kinc  ki  jaka  ki 
puta,  bad  ban  kynmaw  tympang  ieit  ia  ki  rukom  pyrkhat 
uba  rim  ha  ki  spah  bad  hajar  snem  ba  la  lah.  Ki  don 
kiei  kiei  ha  ki  kiba  ring  ban  pynpyrkhat  ia  ki  Nongpule 
bad  la  thoh  ia  ki  ha  ki  ktien  kiba  shai  bad  ba  synlar  eh.  Ki 
kot  kiba  pynpyrkhat  bad  kiba  bat  ia  ka  jingmut  jong  u 
nongpule,  ki  ong,  ba  ki  long  bunsien  kiba  bysa  ia  ka 
jingpyrkhat  bad  kiba  bha  eh  ban  pule.

Ki  Khanatang  ki  ju  iarap  bunsien  ban  khyllie  ia  ka 
Khanapateng  jong  kano  kano  ka  jaid  bynriew.  Kan  poi 
ka  por,  te  ha  kaba  ngin  sa  donkam  ban  suh  Kpieng  lang 
ia  ki  shriak,  la  mynta  ki  long  tang  kum  ki  Symboh  Ksiar 
saphrcd  saphrong  kylleng  sawdong  ka  ri.  Kane  ka  jinglum 
kan  pynkhih  ruh  ia  ka  jingmut  jong  ki  Nongkyndong 
ban  sngewphuhmut  ia  la  ki  Khanatang  ba  ki  la  ioh  lyngba 
bun  pateng,  bad  ban  pynim  pat  ia  ki  ha  la  ki  Ing  bad  ki 
Rympei.  Katno  kan  pynshlur  ia  ki  ynda  ki  la  iohi  ba  ia 
kine  kiba  ki  ju  ia  kren,  la  pynsah  kumne  ha  ka  kitab,  bad 
ban  iohi  ia  ki  ktien  jong  ki  ha  kane  ka  dur.  Ki  ruh  ki  don 
hok  ban  ioh  bynta  lem  ha  kaba  pynkhraw  bad  pyniar  ia 
la  ka  Ktien  da  kaba  pynlang  shuh  shuh  sha  ka  bad  ban 
pynpaw  pyrthei  ia  la  ki  rukom  pyrkhat  ha  ka  thoh  ka  tar.

Ngim  lah  ban  ieit  ia  la  ka  Ri  khlem  da  tip  la  ki 
Khanatang  ki  Longshuwa  jong  ngi.  Ban  bud  dien  bad  ban 
wad  ia  ka  Kpait  ka  rukom  pyrkhat  hyndai,  ka  long  ka 
jingsdang  kaba  shi  kyrdan  sha  ka  jingieit  ri  kaba  shisha.  La 
ki  Nongthoh  bad  ki  Nongiathuh  ki  la  pher,  hynrei  ki 
thymmei  bad  ki  jingthmu  na  kiba  ki  la  rnih  ki  long  ki  juh.

iii

Ki  Khanatang  ki  pher  na  ki  Puriskam  namar  ki  don  ka 
jingiasoh  bad  kiei  kiei  kiba  ki  briew  ki  tip  bad  ki  ngcit 
ba  ki  long  kiba  da  jia  shisha,  kumba  la  iathuh  pateng 
shaphang  jong  ki.  Te,  kim  long  kiba  ngi  lah  ban 
pynphuh  pynphieng  da  ki  shrong  ki  shrup,  bad  ban  suit 
rong  palat,  bad  ban  pynjngai  ia  ki  na  ki  jingshisha  ba  la 
iathuh  bad  ba  ngi  tip  shaphang  jong  ki.  Ki  don  ka 
jingitynnad  ha  ka  jinglong  jong  ki  hi  kumba  la  iathuh  pateng 
ha  ngi.  Namar  kata,  la  thoh  ia  ki  ha  kata  ka  rukom  ha 
kane  ka  kitab.

Ia  U Sier  Lapalang,  uba  ki  khot  U Sier  Laparem 
shaphang  ri  Bhoi,  la  thoh  ha  ka  rukom  Ktien  bad  Kyntien 
Phawar  ha  ki  500  lain.  Ha  kane  ka  Jingphawar,  la  shat 
ki  rukom  im  bad  rukom  pyrkhat  Khasi  kiba  bun  rukom, 
bad  kiba  ngam  shaduh  ki  tlong  ba  bun  syrtap  ka  jinglong 
jong  ngi.  Ngi  ngeit  ba  kane  ka  jingpyrshang  kan  long  ka 
jingprat  lynti  kaba  soh  bad  kaba  sah,  bad  kaba  don  hi 
la  ka  dor.

Ha  ka  Lynnong  kaba  khadduh,  ki  don  kumba  laispah 
duna  ki  4 Ktien  bad  ki  Kyntien  (Words  and  Phrases),  bad  ki 
jingbatai  de,  khnang  ban  iarap  ia  ki  Nongpule.  Lehse 
ngin  sa  ioh  lad  ban  nang  pynbha  bad  pynheh  shuh  shuh  ia 
kine  hadien  habud,  lada  donkam.

Ki  Nongthoh  Kot  ha  kane  ka  ri  jong  ngi  kaba  rit  eh, 
bad  kaba  dang  duna  than  ha  ka  liang  ka  thoh  ka  tar,  ki  shem 
shibun  ki  jingeh  ha  kaba  pynmih  ia  ki  Kot  Thymmai.  Ka 
mynsiem  kaba  khim  ka  dang  synshar  jur  hapdeng  jong  ngi. 
Tang  khyndiat  eh  wat  na  pdeng  ki  Rangbah  kiba  da  kat 
ki  Rangbah  shisha,  kiba  don  ka  jingmut  kaba  iar,  bad  ka

mynsiem  kaba  kylluid,  khnang  ban  roi  ka  thoh  ka  tar  ha

U LAMPHRANGTHAM,  B.A.(Hons).

26-11-1937.

As  a writter  Mr.  P.  Gatphoh  needs  no  introduction 
to  readers.  In  this  book  he  gives  us  not  only  information 
and  entertainment,  but  mental  food  and  discipline  also.

SHI  KYNTIEN

ix

Considering  the  tremendous  trouble  and  patience  that  he 
must  have  borne  in  the  collection  and  siffting  of  facts  and 
materials,  the  author  deserves  a hearty  congratulation  from 
the  reading  public.  The  handling  and  fastidious  pruning 
of  the  matter  contained  are  simply  masterly.  The  book 
does  not  only  instruct  but  would  tend  to  foster  in  the  mind 
a desire  for  culture  and  literary  awakening  through  the 
medium  of  our  own  literature.  Here  we  find  a sincere  attempt 
on  the  part  of  the  author  in  unfolding  the  literary  value  and 
bearing  of  the  past  and  the  antiquarian  remains  with  which 
our  Hills  are  associated  from  time  immemorial.  They 
may  be  mere  legends  but  sometimes  truth  is  stranger  than 
fiction.  They  serve  as  a clue  to  the  study  of  our  olden  peo- 
ple from  all  points  of  view.  We  hear  about  them  but  we  are 
never  familiar  with  what  our  people  could  aspire,  think  and 
accomplish.  The  author  is  conversant  with  the  art  of 
story  telling  in  capturing  the  imagination  of  the  readers, 
which  is  in  itselt  a tour-de-force.  Apart  from  providing 
us  with  what  is  best  in  the  life  of  our  people,  the  book  is 
sure  to  create  an  interest  and  love  for  study  which  are 
conspicuous  by  their  absence.  \n  language  and  diction  it 
is  a model  for  all  simple  and  unadorned  styles  especially 
in  lucidity,  directness,  force  and  felicitous  conveyance  of 
ideas  into  the  most  effective  and  suggestive  words.  It  is  of 
the  student  community  for  the  book  will  surely  supply  their 
long  standing  want.  1 would  like  to  refer  those  who  are 
still  having  their  schooling  and  the  inquisitive  grownups 
to  the  last  chapter  where  the  author  sets  their  difficulties 
at  rest.  The  glossaries  and  explanatory  notes  are  very 
instructive  and  afford  an  easy  way  of  mastering  and

X

SHI  KVNTIEN

construing  Khasi  as  a whole.  Before  concluding  I invite 
the  attention  of  the  readers  to  the  poems  interspersed  in  the 
book.  They  serve  as  a relief  that  diversifies  monotony 
and  relaxes  the  tension  of  the  stories.  In  ‘1/  Sier  Lapalang 
we  have  one  of  finest  narrative  poems  written  after  the 
manner  of  English  narrative  poetry.  The  execution,  crafts- 
manship, and  development  of  the  plot  are  very  ably  brought 
into  the  final  catastrophe  together,  with  side  lights  and  nature 
painting.  The  poem  is  written  in  a five-foot  heroic  verse 
rhyming  in  couple's.  But  there  are  variations  which 
correspond  to  the  rise  and  fall  of  emotions  and  ideas.  In  this 
respect  the  author  has  shown  beyond  doubt  the  metrical  and 
rhythmic  possibilities  of  Khasi.  In  short,  I need  not  dwell 
on  the  merits  of  the  book  as  time  will  be  the  final  judge, 
but  with  confidence  1 am  convinced  that  the  work  is  a 
commendable  one  deserving  a welcome  from  those  for 
whom  it  is  intended.

DATED,  QUAL  A PA  FY,  “j

SHILLONG.  y U DONELTON  PASSAH,  B.  A.
